**Title**: Mirror symmetry for H-polarised Tyurin degenerations of K3 surfaces.

**Structure:**

0. Introduction & statement.
1. Preliminaries on lattices (Λ, H, M; Eichler's criterion; Lemma: primitive embeddings of H; disc forms).
2. Moduli of H-polarised K3 surfaces and their 1-cusps (period domain, Torelli, Baily–Borel; cusps ↔ isotropic planes in L^⊥; cite Fri84, Dol96, Sca87, ABE22).
3. Tyurin degenerations: Kulikov models, Friedman's Torelli-type theorem for degenerations (Fri84: a Type II degeneration is determined by its "d-lattice" data + gluing data; projectivity results SB83).

**Document plan:**

Title: "H-polarised Tyurin degenerations and mirror symmetry with H ⊕ E8 ⊕ E8-quasipolarised K3 surfaces"

§1 Introduction: statement of Main Theorem; strategy; remarks on the correction to [GT24, Def 3.20].

§2 Lattice-theoretic preliminaries.
- Def: Λ_K3, H, M.
- Lemma 2.1 (classification of even unimodular indefinite lattices): statement + citation [Ser70, Ch. V, §2, Thm 5']/[MH73]. Include short proof sketch: uniqueness via neighbours.
- Lemma 2.2: H^⊥ ⊂ Λ ≅ 2H⊕2E8? Actually needed: Λ = H ⊕ H ⊕ M decomposition ✓ trivial.
- Lemma 2.3 (Eichler's criterion): cite [GHS07, Prop 3.3]/[Nik80]. Use for: primitive isotropic vectors, O(M) transitivity.

§3 H-polarised K3 surfaces: moduli and very general members.
- Period domain Ω_H, Torelli [PS71, ...]; moduli as arithmetic quotient; ABE compactification [ABE22]; NL divisors; Lemma: very general H-polarised K3 has NS = j(H). [proof via Torelli + counting, cite Huy16, SD74.]

§4 Tyurin degenerations and cusp data.
- Kulikov/Persson–Pinkham; d-semistability; Friedman's theorem: projectivity & Torelli for Type II [Fri84]; SB83.
- Corrected Definition [GT24, Def 3.20] recalled (as in problem).
- Proposition 4.x (cited [GT24, §3]; sketch): the degeneration determines the cusp lattice N ⊂ H^⊥, N ≅ U; period map extends to ABE compactification with 0 ↦ cusp [N]. Include Remark with the spectral-sequence sketch + the divisibility argument (g_i = 1 via exceptional curves; minimal-pairs impossible) supporting N ≅ U.
- The component lattice data (cite [GT24, §3]): the lattice R(X_0) := ⟨A_0⟩^⊥ ∩ (K_{V_1}^⊥ ⊕ K_{V_2}^⊥).

§5 The mirror lattice.
- Lemma 5.1 (proved): (H ⊕ N)^⊥ ≅ M. [Proof as above: discriminant form + classification. Full details.]
- Def: mirror lattice L^∨ := (L⊕N)^⊥ following [GT24]/[Dol96]-philosophy; remark on Dolgachev.

Given the extreme length of this planning, let me now also decide the statement of the key cited theorem from [GT24] precisely, then write the document. I'll frame it as:

**Theorem A ([GT24], Main Theorem, arXiv:2405.12009).** Let L ⊂ Λ be a primitive lattice polarization and let π: X → Δ be a Tyurin degeneration of L-polarised K3 surfaces satisfying the hypotheses of Definition 3.20 (as corrected above). Suppose π is very general. Let N ⊂ L^⊥ be the cusp lattice of the degeneration [GT24, Constr. 3.x] and let L^∨ := (L ⊕ N)^⊥ ⊂ Λ be the mirror lattice. Then: (i) N ≅ U; (ii) there exists a very general L^∨-quasipolarised K3 surface Y (Dol96), together with an elliptic fibration π: Y → P¹ and an allowable loop γ ⊂ P¹ [GT24, Def 4.11], such that the L^∨-quasipolarisation is compatible with π and γ [GT24, Def 4.21], and (X → Δ; π: Y → P¹, γ) is a mirror pair in the sense of [GT24, Def 5.1]; (iii) in the case L = H, the central fibre has rational elliptic surface components and associated lattice E8⊕E8, and correspondingly the fibration π: Y → P¹ is the fibration attached to the isotropic class of the H-summand of L^∨, with singular fibres of types (II*, II*, I_1, I_1, I_1, I_1) and γ a loop around one of the II* fibres.
